## GHSA-p569-5gjg-9cmj · github.com/rclone/rclone

2026-09-10 · Go · **CRITICAL** 9.1 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the rclone SFTP, FTP, and S3 servers did not correctly handle the authentication proxy configuration. Specifically, when the authentication proxy was enabled, the servers would sometimes fall back to local authentication methods or directly access the VFS without proper proxy validation, allowing an attacker to bypass the intended per-server authentication.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass the authentication proxy and gain unauthorized access to the rclone server, potentially leading to arbitrary file read/write/delete operations on the configured backend storage.

### Key diff

```
--- a/cmd/serve/sftp/server.go
+++ b/cmd/serve/sftp/server.go
@@ -37,28 +37,24 @@ import (
 type server struct {
 	f        fs.Fs
 	opt      Options
-	vfs      *vfs.VFS
+	provider *proxy.Provider
 	ctx      context.Context // for global config
 	config   *ssh.ServerConfig
 	listener net.Listener
 	stopped  chan struct{} // for waiting on the listener to stop
-	proxy    *proxy.Proxy
 }
 
 func newServer(ctx context.Context, f fs.Fs, opt *Options, vfsOpt *vfscommon.Options, proxyOpt *proxy.Options) (*server, error) {
 	s := &server{
-		f:       f,
-		ctx:     ctx,
-		opt:     *opt,
-		stopped: make(chan struct{}),
-	}
-	if proxy.Opt.AuthProxy != "" {
-		s.proxy = proxy.New(ctx, proxyOpt, vfsOpt)
-	} else {
-		s.vfs = vfs.New(ctx, f, vfsOpt)
+		f:        f,
+		ctx:      ctx,
+		opt:      *opt,
+		provider: proxy.NewProvider(ctx, f, vfsOpt, proxyOpt),
+		stopped:  make(chan struct{}),
 	}
 	err := s.configure()
 	if err != nil {
+		s.provider.Shutdown()
 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("sftp configuration failed: %w", err)
 	}
```

### Fix summary

The patch refactors the authentication proxy logic by introducing a `proxy.Provider` struct. This provider encapsulates the VFS and proxy handling, ensuring that all authentication and VFS access consistently go through the proxy when it's enabled, preventing fallback to local authentication or direct VFS access.

## GHSA-jmc6-2wr8-h3wj · open-webui

2026-09-10 · Python · **HIGH** 8.7 · Pattern: `INSECURE_DEFAULT→CONFIG`

### Root cause

The application hardcoded 'allow-same-origin' in the sandbox attribute of an iframe used for port previews. This allowed content loaded within the iframe, even from a different origin (via a proxy), to bypass the same-origin policy and interact with the parent document, leading to a same-origin XSS vulnerability.

### Impact

An attacker could inject malicious scripts into the iframe, which would then be able to access and manipulate the parent document's DOM, steal user credentials (e.g., session tokens), or perform actions on behalf of the user, potentially leading to account takeover.

### Key diff

```
-				sandbox="allow-scripts allow-same-origin allow-forms allow-popups allow-modals allow-downloads"
+				sandbox="allow-scripts{($settings?.terminalPreviewAllowSameOrigin ?? true)
+					? ' allow-same-origin'
+					: ''} allow-forms allow-popups allow-modals allow-downloads"
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new user setting, `terminalPreviewAllowSameOrigin`, which controls whether the 'allow-same-origin' directive is included in the iframe's sandbox attribute. By default, this setting is now configurable, allowing users to disable the 'allow-same-origin' behavior and enhance security.

## GHSA-4v28-j6q3-5m4r · open-webui

2026-09-10 · Python · **HIGH** 7.7 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The Playwright web loader component, when processing user-supplied URLs, relied on the browser to resolve hostnames. This allowed an attacker to use DNS rebinding techniques to bypass initial URL validation checks, which only verified the IP address at the time of connection. After the initial check, a malicious DNS server could resolve the hostname to an internal IP address, leading to server-side request forgery.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass URL validation and make requests to internal services or resources within the network where the Open WebUI application is running, potentially leading to information disclosure or further compromise.

### Key diff

```
--- a/backend/open_webui/retrieval/web/utils.py
+++ b/backend/open_webui/retrieval/web/utils.py
-            validate_url(req.url)
-            resp = route.fetch(max_redirects=0)
+            def fetch(url):
+                validate_url(url)
+                return session.request(
+                    req.method,
+                    url,
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `SafePlaywrightURLLoader` to intercept navigation requests and perform the hostname resolution and URL validation directly within the application's `_SSRFSafeResolver` before allowing the request to proceed. This ensures that all hops, including redirects, are validated against internal IP ranges, preventing DNS rebinding attacks.

## GHSA-4x45-gxvp-6283 · @argos-ci/core

2026-09-10 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The application was constructing shell commands using string interpolation with user-controlled input (e.g., branch names) and executing them via `execSync`. This allowed an attacker to inject arbitrary shell commands by crafting malicious input that would be interpreted as part of the command rather than an argument.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary commands on the system where the application is running, potentially leading to full system compromise, data exfiltration, or denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/core/src/ci-environment/git.ts
+++ b/packages/core/src/ci-environment/git.ts
-    return execSync(`git merge-base ${input.head} ${input.base}`)
+    return execFileSync("git", ["merge-base", input.head, input.base])
```

### Fix summary

The patch replaces `execSync` with `execFileSync` for all `git` commands. `execFileSync` executes a command directly without spawning a shell, treating all arguments as literal values and preventing shell metacharacters from being interpreted.

## GHSA-c476-6w5q-jw77 · github.com/rclone/rclone

2026-09-10 · Go · **HIGH** 7.3 · Pattern: `RACE_CONDITION→TOCTOU`

### Root cause

The FTP server in rclone, when configured with an auth-proxy backend, cached obscured user passwords in a shared map keyed by username. This meant that if two different FTP sessions logged in with the same username but different credentials (resolving to different backend roots via the proxy), the later login would overwrite the cached password for that username, effectively rebinding the earlier session's operations to the later session's backend.

### Impact

An attacker who could log in with a shared username and their own credential could then hijack an existing victim's session using the same username, causing the victim's subsequent FTP operations to be performed against the attacker's controlled backend.

### Key diff

```
--- a/cmd/serve/ftp/ftp.go
+++ b/cmd/serve/ftp/ftp.go
@@ -327,17 +330,18 @@ func (d *driver) CheckPasswd(sctx *ftp.Context, user, pass string) (ok bool, err
 			fs.Infof(nil, "proxy login failed: %v", err)
 			return false, nil
 		}
-		// Cache obscured password for later lookup.
+		// Cache the obscured password on the session for later lookup.
 		//
-		// We don't cache the VFS directly in the driver as we want them
-		// to be expired and the auth proxy does that for us.
+		// We don't cache the VFS directly as we want it to be expired and
+		// the auth proxy does that for us. We bind the credential to this
+		// FTP session rather than to the username so a later login with the
+		// same username but a different credential can't rebind this
+		// session's operations to a different backend.
 		oPass, err := obscure.Obscure(pass)
 		if err != nil {
 			return false, err
 		}
-		d.userPassMu.Lock()
-		d.userPass[user] = oPass
-		d.userPassMu.Unlock()
+		sctx.Sess.Data[sessionObscuredPassKey] = oPass
 	} else {
 		userOK := subtle.ConstantTimeCompare([]byte(d.opt.User), []byte(user))
 		// No password configured means any password is accepted
@@ -361,9 +365,7 @@ func (d *driver) getVFS(sctx *ftp.Context) (VFS *vfs.VFS, err error) {
 		return d.provider.VFS(), nil
 	}
 	user := sctx.Sess.LoginUser()
-	d.userPassMu.Lock()
-	oPass, ok := d.userPass[user]
-	d.userPassMu.Unlock()
+	oPass, ok := sctx.Sess.Data[sessionObscuredPassKey].(string)
 	if !ok {
 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("proxy user not logged in")
 	}
```

### Fix summary

The patch removes the shared `userPass` map and `sync.Mutex` from the `driver` struct. Instead, the obscured password is now stored directly in the `ftp.Session.Data` map, keyed by `sessionObscuredPassKey`. This ensures that each session's credential is bound to that specific session, preventing cross-session credential confusion.

## GHSA-wfgq-w7cq-qj7j · mistralrs-server-core

2026-09-10 · RUST · **HIGH** 7.2 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The application allowed fetching remote URLs for media files without sufficient validation of the target host. Specifically, the `reject_private_host` function, which was intended to prevent access to private or local IP addresses, was not applied to all code paths that could initiate a network request, or it was not robust enough to cover all bypasses. This allowed an attacker to craft a URL that would cause the server to make requests to internal network resources or local files.

### Impact

An attacker could force the server to make requests to arbitrary internal network resources, potentially accessing sensitive data, interacting with internal services, or reading local files from the server's filesystem. This could lead to information disclosure, unauthorized access, or further compromise of the internal infrastructure.

### Key diff

```
--- a/mistralrs-server-core/src/input_files.rs
+++ b/mistralrs-server-core/src/input_files.rs
-    reject_private_host(&parsed)?;
-    let client = reqwest::Client::builder()
-        .timeout(FILE_URL_TIMEOUT)
-        .redirect(reqwest::redirect::Policy::limited(FILE_URL_REDIRECTS))
-        .build()?;
-    let response = client
-        .get(parsed.clone())
-        .send()
-        .await?
-        .error_for_status()?;
+    let parsed = url::Url::parse(url).context("input_file.file_url must be a valid URL")?;
+    let media = fetch_remote_limited(parsed, MAX_INPUT_FILE_BYTES, "input_file.file_url").await?;
+    let filename = media
+        .final_url
+        .as_ref()
+        .and_then(|url| {
+            url.path_segments()
+                .and_then(|mut segments| segments.next_back())
+        })
         .filter(|segment| !segment.is_empty())
         .map(ToString::to_string);
-    Ok((bytes, mime_type, filename))
+    let (bytes, mime_type) = ensure_size(media.bytes, media.mime_type)?;
+    Ok((bytes, mime_type, filename))
```

### Fix summary

The patch refactors the media loading logic into a new `media_source.rs` module. It introduces a centralized `fetch_remote_limited` function that encapsulates robust URL validation, including checks for private IP addresses, loopback addresses, and local hosts, as well as enforcing size and timeout limits. This function is now consistently used for all remote URL fetches, preventing bypasses of the security checks.

## GHSA-2724-6cpj-gf3v · open-webui

2026-09-10 · Python · **HIGH** 7.1 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The application allowed any authenticated user to delete a knowledge base. When an external knowledge base was deleted, the associated external connection was also deleted if it was no longer referenced by other knowledge bases. The critical flaw was that the check for deleting the external connection did not verify if the user initiating the deletion had administrative privileges, even though these connections are admin-owned.

### Impact

A non-admin user could delete external knowledge connections that were created and owned by an administrator. This could lead to denial of service for other users or services relying on these connections, as the external data sources would become inaccessible.

### Key diff

```
--- a/backend/open_webui/routers/knowledge.py
+++ b/backend/open_webui/routers/knowledge.py
@@ -1733,7 +1733,12 @@ async def delete_knowledge_by_id(
     # Clean up vector DB
     if is_external_knowledge(knowledge):
         connection_id = (knowledge.meta or {}).get('external', {}).get('connection_id')
-        if connection_id:
+        # Connections are admin-owned and shared across knowledge bases
+        if (
+            connection_id
+            and user.role == 'admin'
+            and await _count_external_connection_mappings(connection_id, db=db) <= 1
+        ):
             connections = [
                 connection for connection in await _get_external_connections() if connection.get('id') != connection_id
             ]
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds an authorization check to ensure that only users with an 'admin' role can delete external knowledge connections. Additionally, it verifies that the connection is no longer mapped to any other knowledge bases before deletion, preventing accidental removal of shared resources.

## GHSA-34r3-9m95-vq73 · open-webui

2026-09-10 · Python · **HIGH** 7.1 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The application's web fetching utility, intended to restrict access to internal or non-global IP addresses, had an incomplete validation mechanism. Specifically, it failed to properly handle IPv6 addresses that could embed or represent IPv4 addresses, including those used for internal or private networks. This oversight allowed an authenticated attacker to bypass the intended host filtering by crafting URLs that resolved to internal IP addresses via these IPv6 representations.

### Impact

An authenticated attacker could bypass the server-side request forgery (SSRF) protection to access internal network resources, cloud metadata services (like Azure platform channels), or other sensitive endpoints that should not be publicly reachable.

### Key diff

```
--- a/backend/open_webui/retrieval/web/utils.py
+++ b/backend/open_webui/retrieval/web/utils.py
@@ -96,24 +93,37 @@ def _is_global_addr(ip: str) -> bool:
 
     return all(ip.is_global for ip in embedded)
 
+
+def _assert_addresses_allowed(addresses: Sequence[str]) -> None:
+    # An IPv6 address can carry a blocked IPv4 address inside it, so judge both spellings.
+    parsed = [ipaddress.ip_address(address) for address in addresses]
+    candidates = [*parsed, *(ipv4 for address in parsed for ipv4 in _embedded_ipv4(address))]
+
+    # Block entries only: an allow entry names a host, so judging a resolved address against one
+    # would reject every allow-listed host.
+    if is_host_blocked([str(address) for address in candidates], WEB_FETCH_FILTER_LIST):
+        log.warning(f'Blocked by filter list: {', '.join(str(address) for address in candidates)}')
+        raise ValueError(ERROR_MESSAGES.INVALID_URL)
+
+    if not ENABLE_LOCAL_WEB_FETCH:
+        for address in candidates:
+            if not address.is_global:
+                log.warning(f'Blocked non-global address: {address}')
+                raise ValueError(ERROR_MESSAGES.INVALID_URL)
 
 def validate_url(url: Union[str, Sequence[str]]):
     if not url:
         raise ValueError(ERROR_MESSAGES.INVALID_URL)
 
     if isinstance(url, str):
         parsed_url = urlparse(url)
         if parsed_url.scheme not in ['http', 'https']:
             log.warning(f'Blocked non-HTTP(S) protocol: {parsed_url.scheme} in URL: {url}')
             raise ValueError(ERROR_MESSAGES.INVALID_URL)
 
-        # Blocklist check using unified filtering logic
-        if WEB_FETCH_FILTER_LIST:
-            # Match on the parsed hostname, not the full URL: a path component would
-            # otherwise let any URL slip past a hostname-based block/allow entry.
-            if not is_host_allowed(parsed_url.hostname, WEB_FETCH_FILTER_LIST):
-                log.warning(f'URL blocked by filter list: {url}')
-                raise ValueError(ERROR_MESSAGES.INVALID_URL)
+        _assert_host_allowed(parsed_url.hostname)
 
         # Validate the hostname and resolve to IP addresses
         try:
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces more robust IP address validation by explicitly checking for embedded IPv4 addresses within IPv6 addresses. It also separates host-based allow/block list checks from IP address-based checks and ensures that both the hostname and all resolved IP addresses (including embedded ones) are validated against the filter lists and global address requirements. A new `is_host_blocked` function was added to specifically check against block entries.

---

## GHSA-4qg5-cxx4-g927 · open-webui

2026-09-10 · Python · **MODERATE** 6.5 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The token exchange endpoint, designed to allow users to sign in via an OAuth token, did not enforce the configured OAuth domain allowlist or role-based access policies. This oversight meant that even if a user's email domain was explicitly denied or their role did not grant access through the standard OAuth flow, they could still gain access by directly using the token exchange mechanism.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass the configured domain allowlist or role-based access controls, allowing unauthorized users to sign in to the application. This could lead to unauthorized access to sensitive information or functionality.

### Key diff

```
--- a/backend/open_webui/routers/auths.py
+++ b/backend/open_webui/routers/auths.py
@@ -1295,6 +1296,17 @@ async def token_exchange(
         )
     email = email.lower()
 
+    # Enforce domain allowlist — same check as the normal OAuth callback
+    if (
+        '*' not in auth_manager_config.OAUTH_ALLOWED_DOMAINS
+        and email.split('@')[-1] not in auth_manager_config.OAUTH_ALLOWED_DOMAINS
+    ):
+        log.warning(f'Token exchange denied: email domain not in allowed domains list')
+        raise HTTPException(
+            status_code=status.HTTP_403_FORBIDDEN,
+            detail=ERROR_MESSAGES.ACCESS_PROHIBITED,
+        )
+
     # Try to find the user by OAuth sub
     user = await Users.get_user_by_oauth_sub(provider, sub, db=db)
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds a check to the token exchange endpoint to enforce the OAuth domain allowlist. It verifies if the user's email domain is present in the `OAUTH_ALLOWED_DOMAINS` configuration, similar to the check performed during the normal OAuth callback, and denies access if the domain is not allowed.

## GHSA-38xv-hf3p-h7mq · github.com/rclone/rclone

2026-09-10 · Go · **MODERATE** 5.3 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The rclone application did not properly sanitize or validate object names returned by remote storage systems. Malicious or buggy servers could provide object names containing path traversal sequences (e.g., ".."), which, when joined with the configured root path, could cause operations to escape the intended directory confinement.

### Impact

An attacker, by controlling the object names returned by a remote storage system, could cause rclone to list, transfer, or stat files outside of the configured root directory, potentially leading to unauthorized data access or modification.

### Key diff

```
--- a/fs/list/list.go
+++ b/fs/list/list.go
@@ -13,6 +14,39 @@ import (
 	"github.com/rclone/rclone/lib/bucket"
 )
 
+// RemoteEscapesRoot reports whether remote, taken as a path relative to an Fs
+// root, climbs above that root when joined onto it.
+func RemoteEscapesRoot(remote string) bool {
+	const sentinel = "\x00rootsentinel"
+	joined := path.Join(sentinel, remote)
+	return joined != sentinel && !strings.HasPrefix(joined, sentinel+"/")
+}
+
+// RemoveEscaping drops - and logs - any entries whose Remote escapes the Fs
+// root (see RemoteEscapesRoot), filtering the slice in place.
+func RemoveEscaping(entries fs.DirEntries) fs.DirEntries {
+	kept := entries[:0]
+	for _, entry := range entries {
+		if RemoteEscapesRoot(entry.Remote()) {
+			fs.Errorf(entry, "Entry %q escapes the root - ignoring", entry.Remote())
+			continue
+		}
+		kept = append(kept, entry)
+	}
+	return kept
+}
+
 // DirSorted reads Object and *Dir into entries for the given Fs.
 //
 // dir is the start directory, "" for root
@@ -99,6 +133,7 @@ func DirSortedFn(ctx context.Context, f fs.Fs, includeAll bool, dir string, call
 func filterDir(ctx context.Context, entries fs.DirEntries, includeAll bool, dir string,
 	IncludeObject func(ctx context.Context, o fs.Object) bool,
 	IncludeDirectory func(remote string) (bool, error)) (newEntries fs.DirEntries, err error) {
+	entries = RemoveEscaping(entries)
 	newEntries = entries[:0] // in place filter
 	prefix := ""
 	if dir != "" {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new function, `RemoteEscapesRoot`, which checks if a remote path attempts to climb above a given root. This function is then integrated into listing and stat operations (`RemoveEscaping` and direct checks) to filter out any entries that escape the configured root, preventing them from being processed.

## GHSA-3pf7-q2g3-wj28 · open-webui

2026-09-10 · Python · **MODERATE** 4.3 · Pattern: `IDOR→DATA_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The application allowed authenticated users to specify a `folder_id` when creating or updating chats. While there were checks to ensure the `folder_id` belonged to the user or was a shared folder with write access, these checks were not consistently applied or were implemented in a verbose, repetitive manner. Specifically, the `chat_completion` endpoint, which handles the core chat creation logic, lacked a proper authorization check for the `folder_id` when a new chat was being saved, allowing an attacker to inject chats into another user's folder.

### Impact

An authenticated attacker could inject new chat entries into any existing folder, including those belonging to other users, by supplying the target folder's ID. This could lead to unauthorized data manipulation and potentially confusion or disruption for other users.

### Key diff

```
--- a/backend/open_webui/routers/chats.py
+++ b/backend/open_webui/routers/chats.py
@@ -753,20 +753,11 @@ async def create_new_chat(
     user=Depends(get_verified_user),
     db: AsyncSession = Depends(get_async_session),
 ):
-    # Reject a folder_id that doesn't belong to the caller.
-    if form_data.folder_id is not None:
-        if not await Folders.get_folder_by_id_and_user_id(form_data.folder_id, user.id, db=db):
-            # Check shared folder write access
-            shared_folder = await Folders.get_folder_by_id(form_data.folder_id, db=db)
-            if not shared_folder or not await has_folder_access(user.id, shared_folder, 'write', db):
-                raise HTTPException(
-                    status_code=status.HTTP_404_NOT_FOUND,
-                    detail=ERROR_MESSAGES.NOT_FOUND,
-                )
+    if form_data.folder_id is not None and not await has_folder_write_access(user.id, form_data.folder_id, db=db):
+        raise HTTPException(
+            status_code=status.HTTP_404_NOT_FOUND,
+            detail=ERROR_MESSAGES.NOT_FOUND,
+        )
 
     try:
         chat = await Chats.insert_new_chat(str(uuid4()), user.id, form_data, db=db)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new helper function `has_folder_write_access` to centralize and simplify the authorization check for folder write permissions. This function is then consistently applied across all relevant endpoints (`create_new_chat`, `update_chat_folder_id_by_id`, and `chat_completion`) to ensure that any provided `folder_id` is properly validated for write access by the current user.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 23 occurrences**
> Languages: JavaScript, Go, PHP, Python, Java

## GHSA-fmqh-xp37-5hr8 · open-webui

2026-09-10 · Python · **MODERATE** 4.3 · Pattern: `IDOR→DATA_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The application allowed channel members with write access to overwrite any message within that channel, regardless of whether they were the original author of the message. The authorization check only verified write access to the channel, not authorship of the specific message being modified.

### Impact

An attacker who is a member of a channel could overwrite messages posted by other members in the same channel, leading to message tampering and potential misinformation.

### Key diff

```
if target_message and (
                        target_message.channel_id != channel.id
                        # Write access is not authorship — block cross-member edits.
                        or (user.role != 'admin' and target_message.user_id != user.id)
                    ):
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds an additional check to the chat_completion endpoint. When a message is being modified in a channel, it now verifies that the user attempting the modification is either an administrator or the original author of the message, preventing cross-member message overwrites.

## GHSA-pcvc-8vrv-8q6w · open-webui

2026-09-10 · Python · **MODERATE** 4.3 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from the vector database backends (Milvus, OpenGauss, Qdrant) not properly filtering knowledge base access based on user permissions. When a user performed a search, the system would query the vector database without applying necessary access control filters, allowing the built-in knowledge tool to expose information from knowledge bases that the user should not have been able to access.

### Impact

An attacker could query the system to retrieve information from knowledge bases they are not authorized to view, leading to unauthorized data access and information disclosure.

### Key diff

```
--- a/backend/open_webui/retrieval/vector/dbs/milvus.py
+++ b/backend/open_webui/retrieval/vector/dbs/milvus.py
@@ -201,6 +201,7 @@ def search(
             data=vectors,
             limit=limit,
             output_fields=['data', 'metadata'],
+            **kwargs,
             # search_params=search_params # Potentially add later if needed
         )
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces explicit filtering mechanisms for search operations in Milvus, OpenGauss, and Qdrant clients. It adds `_metadata_exprs` and `_metadata_filter` functions to construct proper filter conditions based on user permissions, ensuring that search queries only return results from accessible knowledge bases.

## GHSA-f52w-8j3h-j724 · github.com/traefik/traefik/v2

2026-09-10 · Go · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `TYPE_CONFUSION→BYPASS`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from Traefik's handling of HTTP/1 request-targets, specifically opaque URLs. When an opaque URL was received, Go's URL parsing populated the 'Opaque' field and left 'Path', 'RawPath', and 'Host' empty. This caused Traefik's path-scoped routing and middleware to treat the request as '/', effectively bypassing security checks, while the original opaque target was reinstated when forwarding to the backend.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass path-scoped routing, middleware guards, and access logging by crafting requests with opaque URLs. This could lead to unauthorized access to internal services or resources that would otherwise be protected by Traefik's routing rules and security policies.

### Key diff

```
--- a/pkg/server/server_entrypoint_tcp.go
+++ b/pkg/server/server_entrypoint_tcp.go
@@ -649,6 +649,10 @@ func createHTTPServer(ctx context.Context, ln net.Listener, configuration *stati
 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("invalid aliasHeadersStrategy value %q", configuration.HTTP.AliasHeadersStrategy)
 	}
 
+	// An opaque URL has to be rejected before any handler deriving the path or the request URI from it,
+	// hence the wrapping has to be done last so that it is the first handler executed.
+	handler = denyOpaque(handler)
+
 	var connContext multipleConnContext
 	connContext.AddConnContextFunc(func(ctx context.Context, c net.Conn) context.Context {
 		// This adds an empty struct in order to store a RoundTripper in the ConnContext in case of Kerberos or NTLM.
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new handler, `denyOpaque`, which is executed early in the request processing pipeline to reject any incoming requests with opaque URLs. Additionally, it explicitly clears the `Opaque` field in outgoing requests to ensure that backend services do not receive malformed or unexpected URLs.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 7 occurrences**
> Languages: JavaScript, RUST, Erlang, Go
> → GHSA-g8f2-4f4f-5jqw
> → GHSA-429q-fhh4-r6hj
> → GHSA-6j2x-vhqr-qr7q
> → GHSA-7rqj-j65f-68wh
> → GHSA-mwr4-5g34-j5cq
> → GHSA-5jgf-p345-68v8

## GHSA-fx5j-qcqg-grpf · github.com/xuri/excelize

2026-09-10 · Go · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `INTEGER_OVERFLOW→BOUNDARY`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from improper validation of input values, specifically a shared-string index and row numbers. A negative shared-string index or an excessively large row number could lead to out-of-bounds access or resource exhaustion, causing the application to panic or behave unexpectedly.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious Excel file that, when processed by the library, would cause the application to crash due to a panic, leading to a denial of service for users of the application.

### Key diff

```
--- a/cell.go
+++ b/cell.go
@@ -623,9 +623,10 @@ func (c *xlsxC) getValueFrom(f *File, d *xlsxSST, raw bool) (string, error) {
 			}
 			d.mu.Lock()
 			defer d.mu.Unlock()
-			if len(d.SI) > xlsxSI {
-				return f.formattedValue(&xlsxC{S: c.S, V: d.SI[xlsxSI].String()}, raw, CellTypeSharedString)
+			if xlsxSI < 0 || xlsxSI >= len(d.SI) {
+				return "", newInvalidSharedStringIndex(xlsxSI)
 			}
+			return f.formattedValue(&xlsxC{S: c.S, V: d.SI[xlsxSI].String()}, raw, CellTypeSharedString)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces explicit checks for the shared-string index to ensure it is non-negative and within the bounds of the shared string array. It also adds a check for row numbers to prevent them from exceeding a defined maximum, returning an error instead of allowing a panic.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 18 occurrences**
> Languages: Go, C#, RUST, PHP, Python, JavaScript

## GHSA-hf57-cqmx-p4gr · omniroute

2026-09-10 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from insufficient validation of user-controlled input in the `versionCommand` argument for custom agents. Specifically, the system allowed arbitrary arguments to be passed to an interpreter during a version probe, which could include eval-style arguments (e.g., `node -e '...'`), leading to code execution without requiring shell metacharacters.

### Impact

An attacker could register a malicious custom agent and, by crafting a specific `versionCommand`, achieve arbitrary code execution on the server where OmniRoute is running.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/lib/acp/registry.ts
+++ b/src/lib/acp/registry.ts
@@ -300,6 +308,12 @@ export function resolveVersionProbe(
     if (!allowed.has(normalizedCommand)) {
       return null;
     }
+
+    // Untrusted probe: allow only a bare binary or a single recognized version
+    // flag, so a matching interpreter cannot smuggle an eval/exec argument.
+    if (args.length > 1 || (args.length === 1 && !SAFE_VERSION_PROBE_ARG.test(args[0]))) {
+      return null;
+    }
   }
 
   return { command, args };
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a strict validation for `versionCommand` arguments in custom agent version probes. It now only permits a bare binary or a single, recognized version flag (e.g., `-v`, `--version`), preventing the injection of arbitrary commands or eval-style arguments. Additionally, it addresses several authorization bypasses by ensuring case-insensitive matching for API paths and adding critical database backup paths to always-protected lists.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 94 occurrences**

## GHSA-v67p-phpq-fc8x · github.com/traefik/traefik/v3

2026-09-10 · Go · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→HEADER`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because Traefik's header-name sanitization logic, which is applied to incoming request headers, did not account for request trailers. Attackers could use request trailers to bypass these sanitization rules, as trailers are processed after initial routing and security decisions are made based on the (sanitized) headers.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially inject unsanitized header names into backend requests, which might lead to various issues depending on the backend application's handling of such headers, including potential information disclosure or unexpected behavior.

### Key diff

```
--- a/pkg/proxy/httputil/proxy.go
+++ b/pkg/proxy/httputil/proxy.go
@@ -107,6 +107,19 @@ func rewriteRequestBuilder(target *url.URL, passHostHeader bool, preservePath bo
 		// URL.RequestURI gives Opaque precedence over the path, an opaque outgoing URL would discard the path set above.
 		pr.Out.URL.Opaque = ""
 
+ 		// Forward the declared request trailer names, but never their values: they arrive
+ 		// after the header section, once routing and security decisions are made, and would
+ 		// otherwise reach the backend under a name sanitized in the header section.
+ 		// Discarding them is allowed by https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9112#section-7.1.2,
+ 		// and the names are kept as the hint of what was dropped described in
+ 		// https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-6.6.2
+ 		// Emptying the outgoing map is what makes this hold whatever the middleware chain did
+ 		// to the incoming request, as the transport only writes pr.Out.Trailer.
+ 		// Response trailers are unaffected.
+ 		for name := range pr.Out.Trailer {
+ 			pr.Out.Trailer[name] = nil
+ 		}
+
 		pr.Out.Proto = "HTTP/1.1"
 		pr.Out.ProtoMajor = 1
 		pr.Out.ProtoMinor = 1
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the vulnerability by explicitly clearing all request trailer values before forwarding the request to the backend. It iterates through the `pr.Out.Trailer` map and sets all trailer values to `nil`, effectively discarding their content while keeping the names as a hint.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 16 occurrences**
> Languages: JavaScript, PHP, Python, Erlang, Go, Java
> → GHSA-fvcv-3m26-pcqx
> → GHSA-247c-9743-5963
> → GHSA-c3h8-g69v-pjrg
> → GHSA-gv7r-3mr9-h5x8
> → GHSA-36hh-x5p5-jgc8
> → GHSA-86qp-5c8j-p5mr
> → GHSA-3hrh-pfw6-9m5x
> → GHSA-m6qw-4cw2-hm4m
> → GHSA-f9vr-g2g2-x9fg
> → GHSA-pqg7-v6wh-3pfp
> → GHSA-4qhr-g3c6-fcfx
> → GHSA-px9f-whj3-246m
> → GHSA-4cwx-7wf7-3272

> → GHSA-w4v4-9rw7-5326

## GHSA-w4v4-9rw7-5326 · github.com/traefik/traefik/v2

2026-09-10 · Go · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→HEADER`

### Root cause

Traefik, acting as a reverse proxy, was forwarding client-initiated h2c upgrade headers to backend services. While Traefik itself no longer honored these h2c upgrade requests (since Go 1.24), the underlying httputil.ReverseProxy library continued to forward the 'Upgrade' and 'Http2-Settings' headers. This inconsistency between Traefik's interpretation and the backend's potential interpretation could lead to request smuggling.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially bypass security controls, gain unauthorized access to internal resources, or manipulate backend application logic by smuggling requests. This could lead to data breaches, unauthorized actions, or further compromise of the system.

### Key diff

```
--- /dev/null
+++ b/pkg/server/service/upgrade.go
@@ -0,0 +1,37 @@
+...
+func (h *h2cUpgradeHandler) ServeHTTP(rw http.ResponseWriter, req *http.Request) {
+	if httpguts.HeaderValuesContainsToken(req.Header["Connection"], "Upgrade") &&
+		httpguts.HeaderValuesContainsToken([]string{req.Header.Get("Upgrade")}, "h2c") {
+		delete(req.Header, "Upgrade")
+	}
+	delete(req.Header, "Http2-Settings")
+...

--- a/pkg/server/service/proxy.go
+++ b/pkg/server/service/proxy.go
@@ -148,5 +148,5 @@ func buildProxy(passHostHeader *bool, responseForwarding *dynamic.ResponseForwar
 		},
 	}
 
-	return newConnectHandler(proxy), nil
+	return newConnectHandler(newH2CUpgradeHandler(proxy)), nil
 }
```

### Fix summary

A new `h2cUpgradeHandler` was introduced to explicitly remove the 'Upgrade' and 'Http2-Settings' headers from incoming requests if they indicate an h2c upgrade. This handler is now wrapped around the main proxy, ensuring these headers are stripped before the request reaches the backend services, thus preventing inconsistent interpretation.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 16 occurrences**

> → GHSA-247c-9743-5963
> → GHSA-c3h8-g69v-pjrg

> → GHSA-36hh-x5p5-jgc8

> → GHSA-3hrh-pfw6-9m5x
> → GHSA-m6qw-4cw2-hm4m
> → GHSA-f9vr-g2g2-x9fg
> → GHSA-pqg7-v6wh-3pfp
> → GHSA-4qhr-g3c6-fcfx
